# Existing application gap analysis

> Repository cleanup, 8 November 2026: this is historical source analysis. Unused legacy modules and older audits are retained in the local external backup; their former links are marked as archived. See current acceptance for active behavior.

> Current implementation: the later workflow-correction brief authorized application changes after this Phase 1 proposal. See [current acceptance and remaining work](../workflow-correction/ACCEPTANCE_STATUS.md). The original blueprint and its historical approval boundary below are preserved; deployment and production migrations remain separate.


Phase 1 • Product blueprint and source comparison • 5 November 2026

## Comparison boundary and evidence

The target in documents 01–12 was defined before this comparison. The current application is evidence, not the ideal. No app implementation was changed. This is a comprehensive static inventory comparison with bounded prior runtime evidence; it is not an exhaustive production test or a certification of security. Product additions such as outcome follow-up and private contact sharing are proposals, not newly discovered bugs.

Read-only reinspection covered App routes and all 52 finding anchors, request intake, field mapping, Firestore access, Functions deletion and identity paths. The [source baseline](source-baseline.json) covers 516 inventoried source/config/test/script/public files; the final [integrity result](source-integrity.json) reports changes against this phase's baseline. [Anchor validation](finding-anchor-validation.json) tests exact stored line excerpts. Historical screenshots/browser results remain in `the previous audit` (archived during repository cleanup); no new browser, emulator, lint or test run is represented here.

The prior audit observed 24 rendered route/viewport combinations (8 routes × 390/768/1440), targeted landing search/city behavior and six read-only seeded admin record comparisons. No overflow/page errors there does not establish functional correctness. A seeded chat fixture was unavailable, so chat partner navigation is source-confirmed and runtime-unverified. Rules exposure, data-attribute injection, review races, deletion effects and deployed abuse controls retain their prior limitations; no production exploit or private production record was inspected.

Historical test evidence: 430 assertions passed across 38 passing source test files, with 3 additional files failing missing-component imports; the full unit gate failed. Four Functions stub tests passed. Historical lint reported 552 errors and 2 warnings. These are inherited results on the inspected source snapshot, not execution in this blueprint phase.

## Classification rules

Correctly implemented means a bounded named behavior meets its target with adequate evidence. Partially implemented means part exists but acceptance is incomplete/unverified. Incorrectly implemented contradicts a current behavior promise. Inconsistent means two sections disagree. Missing means no target path is present. Redundant means overlapping engines/contracts should converge, not blind deletion. Unnecessary means no justified core purpose or a false operation. Disconnected means supporting code/data exists without a complete active journey. Unverified means evidence cannot establish runtime behavior. No whole feature is marked correctly implemented solely because the prior recommendation was KEEP. Existing successful Auth, bookmark or decision primitives are retained inside partially complete end-to-end journeys.

Authentication/setup are modal or internal modes, not independent durable routes. Notifications are an internal view. P08/P09, nested conversation/editor/applicant views and public help/legal destinations have no concrete route definitions; the target routes are proposals. The 22 target view specs are logical experiences, not a claim that 22 route modules exist or should all become separate visual pages.

## Confirmed findings and bounded risks

The 52 entries are inherited findings with rechecked anchors: 26 confirmed defects, 9 missing-functionality findings, 4 logical concerns, 7 security/privacy risks and 6 code-quality issues; 38 P1 and 14 P2. Prior verification labels describe the named evidence, often static verification, not 52 runtime confirmations. Risk labels are not proof of exploitation. Exact actual behavior, target linkage and dependencies are retained below; original observed behavior, impact and limitations remain in `audit-findings.json` (archived during repository cleanup).

400 literal button/input/select/textarea/link source occurrences are indexed below and with surrounding source in [element-traceability.json](element-traceability.json). Loops, responsive copies, template strings and dormant modules make this different from a count of unique rendered controls. The smallest enclosing inventoried function is shown where available. Candidate feature/page ownership is file-level, not an invented exact handler mapping. Every occurrence remains runtime Unverified at this granularity; the feature tables, 111 distinct inventoried action hooks, and P/T specifications supply the reviewed semantic contract. This limitation must be resolved with branch-by-branch UI walkthrough before declaring element-level implementation acceptance complete.

All 32 prior reconstructed W workflows are separately mapped here; W identifiers describe existing journeys and are not aliases for the independently designed T transitions. Original bounded status is preserved alongside the target classification. Rows with no attached defect use the active Auth/Explore/backend entry modules; a partial rating is not a complete runtime pass.
